Definition 9.6.59. The topology on M whose closed sets are exactly the semialgebraic arc-
symmetric subsets of M is called the AS topology on M. If S is a subset of M, the closure
of S with respect to AS topology is denoted by S
AS.
Remark 9.6.60. The AS topology on the Nash manifold Rn is the AR topology on Rn.
Any irreducible AS-closed set of M will be called an AS-irreducible set and, since the AS
topology is Noetherian, any AS-closed set of M has a unique finite decomposition into AS-
irreducible sets that we will call its AS-irreducible components.
Like the AR topology on real affine spaces (proposition 9.6.18), we have:
Lemma 9.6.61. Any AS-irreducible set of M is connected with respect to the (strong) topology
of M.
Proof. Let S be a AS-closed irreducible set of M and S1, . . . , Sk be its connected components,
which are semialgebraic (by corollary 9.6.50) and arc-symmetric (see remark 9.6.37), i.e. AS-
closed in M. Therefore, since S = ⊔k
i=1Si is AS-irreducible, we have k = 1 and S = S1 is
connected.
Remark 9.6.62. Since there is no well-defined notion of “real algebraic subset” for a Nash
manifold, we cannot consider the “Zariski closure” of a subset of M, whereas this was crucial
to prove that the dimension of the AR-closure of a semialgebraic subset of Rn is equal to the
dimension of the latter.
Recall that a compact Nash submanifold of M is itself a Nash manifold (remark 9.6.51)
and it is therefore natural to investigate the relation between the two associated AS topology.
Proposition 9.6.63.
Let N be a compact Nash submanifold of M. The AS topology on the
induced Nash manifold N coincides with the topology induced on N by the AS topology on M.
Proof. If S is a subset of N, then S is semialgebraic in N if and only if S is semialgebraic
in M by lemma 9.6.45 3 (applied to the inclusion map N ,→M which is Nash) and, because N
is in particular a closed analytic submanifold of M, S is arc-symmetric in N if and only if S
is arc-symmetric in M by lemma 9.6.40. As a consequence, the AS-closed sets of the Nash
manifold N are exactly the subsets of N obtained by intersecting the AS-closed sets of M
with N (N is itself an AS-closed set of M).
112


## Page 113


As in the case of real affine spaces (proposition 9.6.30), we can establish the “compatibility”
of AS-closed sets of Nash manifolds with semialgebraic arc-analytic maps:
Proposition 9.6.64. Let S be an AS-closed set of M, T be a semialgebraic subset of a Nash
manifold N and f : S →T be a semialgebraic arc-analytic map.
1. If T is AS-closed in N, the graph Γf := {(x, y) ∈S × T | y = f(x)} of f is AS-closed in
M × N.
2. For any AS-closed set A of N included in T, the set f−1(A) is AS-closed in M, i.e. f
is continuous with respect to (the topologies induced on S and T by) AS topology.
In particular, a Nash diffeomorphism between Nash manifolds is an homeomorphism with re-
spect to AS topology.
Proof.
1. By lemma 9.6.47 1, the graph Γf is semialgebraic in M × N since f is semial-
gebraic.
If T is arc-symmetric in N, Γf is furthermore arc-symmetric in M × N by
proposition 9.6.39 1.
2. If A is an AS-closed set of N included in T, f−1(A) is semialgebraic in M by lemma 9.6.47 2
and arc-symmetric in M by proposition 9.6.39 2.
Remarks 9.6.65.
1. Suppose that M is an affine Nash manifold, i.e. that there exists a Nash diffeomorphism
Ψ from M to a Nash submanifold L of a real affine space Rs (we will call such a map an
affine Nash embedding of M). Suppose furthermore that M is compact (L is therefore a
compact Nash submanifold of Rs). Then, according to proposition 9.6.64 and proposition
9.6.63, if S is a subset of M, the set S is AS-closed in M if and only if Ψ(S) is AR-closed
in Rs.
2. Any semialgebraic arc-analytic map between AS-closed sets of compact affine Nash man-
ifolds is continuous with respect to the strong topologies induced by the latter (compose
the considered map with respective affine Nash embeddings of the considered compact
Nash manifolds and use proposition 9.6.30 1).
9.6.8
Quasi-arc-symmetric sets and AS-sets
In this paragraph, we will consider, together with the semialgebraic arc-symmetric sets of the
Nash manifold M, two more general classes of objets, namely the quasi-arc-symmetric sets
of M and the AS-sets of M. We will show that when M is compact and affine, the two notions
coincide. From now on, we will focus on semialgebraic subsets of M.
Definition 9.6.66. Let S be a semialgebraic subset of M.
We say that S is a quasi-arc-
symmetric set of M if for any analytic arc γ :] −1; 1[→M, if γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂S, then there exists
ϵ ∈]0; 1[ such that γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂S.
113


## Page 114


Remark 9.6.67. By arguments similar to the proof of lemma 9.6.3, a semialgebraic subset S
of M is quasi-arc-symmetric if and only if for any analytic arc γ :]ϵ; ϵ′[→M,
(
if γ(]a, b[) ⊂S with ϵ ≤a < b < ϵ′, then there exists ξ ∈]b; ϵ′[ such that γ(]b; ξ[) ⊂S,
if γ(]a, b[) ⊂S with ϵ < a < b ≤ϵ′, then there exists ξ ∈]ϵ; a[ such that γ(]ξ; a[) ⊂S.
Of course, an AS-closed set of M is a quasi-arc-symmetric set, which is furthermore closed
(for the strong topology of M) by proposition 9.6.56. The converse is also true:
Lemma 9.6.68. A quasi-arc-symmetric set S of M is arc-symmetric in M if and only if it is
closed for the (strong) topology of M.
Proof. Suppose that S is a closed subset of M and let γ :] −1; 1[→M be an analytic arc
such that γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂S: since S is quasi-arc-symmetric in M, there exists ϵ ∈]0; 1[ such
that γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂S. But S is closed in M so that γ(0) ∈γ

] −1; 0[

⊂γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂S. As a
consequence, γ(] −1; ϵ[) ⊂S and S is then arc-symmetric in M.
Like AS-closed sets, the class of quasi-arc-symmetric sets of M is stable under finite inter-
section and finite union. But it is stable under set-theoritic difference as well:
Proposition 9.6.69. Any finite Boolean combination of quasi-arc-symmetric subsets of M is
quasi-arc-symmetric in M.
Proof. Recall lemma 9.6.44 and let A and B be quasi-arc-symmetric subsets of M. Let γ :
] −1; 1[→M be an analytic arc.
First, suppose that γ(]−1; 0[) ⊂A∩B. Since A and B are quasi-arc-symmetric, there exist
ϵ, ϵ′ ∈]0; 1[ such that γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂A and γ(]0; ϵ′[) ⊂B and then, if we denote by ϵ′′ the minimum
of ϵ and ϵ′, we have γ(]0; ϵ′′[) ⊂A ∩B. As a consequence, A ∩B is quasi-arc-symmetric in M.
Now, suppose that γ(]−1; 0[) ⊂A∪B. By lemma 9.6.70 below, there exists ξ ∈]−1; 0[ such
that γ(]ξ; 0[) ⊂A or there exists ξ ∈] −1; 0[ such that γ(]ξ; 0[) ⊂B. As a consequence, since A
and B are quasi-arc-symmetric in M, there exists ϵ ∈]0; 1[ such that γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂A ⊂A ∪B or
there exists ϵ ∈]0; 1[ such that γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂B ⊂A ∪B: the union A ∪B is therefore quasi-arc-
symmetric in M.
Finally, suppose that γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂A \ B. Since A is quasi-arc-symmetric in M, there
exists ϵ0 ∈]0; 1[ such that γ(]0; ϵ0[) ⊂A. Now, suppose by absurd that for any ϵ ∈]0; ϵ0[, there
exists tϵ ∈]0; ϵ[ such that γ(tϵ) ∈B: we can adapt the arguments of the proof of lemma 9.6.70
below to assert that there then exists ϵ′ ∈]0; ϵ0[ such that ]0; ϵ′[⊂γ−1(B). Therefore, since B is
quasi-arc-symmetric in M, there is ξ ∈] −1; 0[ such that γ(]ξ; 0[) ⊂B. But this contradicts the
inclusion γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂A \ B. Consequently, there exists ϵ ∈]0; ϵ0[ such that γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂A \ B
and, as a result, A \ B is quasi-arc-symmetric in M.
Lemma 9.6.70. Let γ :]−1; 1[→M be an analytic arc and let A and B be distinct semialgebraic
subsets of M such that γ(]−1; 0[) ⊂A∪B. Then there exists ξ ∈]−1; 0[ such that γ(]ξ; 0[) ⊂A
or there exists ξ ∈] −1; 0[ such that γ(]ξ; 0[) ⊂B.

Remark 9.6.71.
If S is a quasi-arc-symmetric set of M and T is a quasi-arc-symmetric set of
a Nash manifold N, then the cartesian product S × T is a quasi-arc-symmetric set of the Nash
manifold M × N. Indeed, we have lemma 9.6.44 and, if γ = (γ1, γ2) :] −1; 1[→M × N is an
analytic arc satisfying γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂S × T i.e. γ1(] −1; 0[) ⊂S and γ2(] −1; 0[) ⊂T, then,
since S and T are quasi-arc-symmetric, there exist ϵ1 > 0, ϵ2 > 0 such that γ1(]0; ϵ1[) ⊂S
and γ2(]0; ϵ2[) ⊂T. As a consequence, if we set ϵ to be the minimum of ϵ1 and ϵ2, we have
γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂S × T.
Quasi-arc-symmetric sets are also stable under inverse image by semialgebraic arc-analytic
maps:
Lemma 9.6.72.
Let S be an AS-closed set of M, T be a semialgebraic set of a Nash mani-
fold N and f : S →T be a semialgebraic arc-analytic map. If A is a quasi-arc-symmetric set
of N included in T, then f−1(A) is quasi-arc-symmetric in M.
Proof. If γ :]−1; 1[→M is an analytic arc such that γ(]−1; 0[) ⊂f−1(A) ⊂S, then γ(]−1; 1[) ⊂
S (since S is arc-symmetric in M), the analytic arc f ◦γ :]−1; 1[→N satisfies f ◦γ(]−1; 0[) ⊂A
and, since A is quasi-arc-symmetric in N, there exists ϵ ∈]0; 1[ such that f ◦γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂A i.e.
γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂f−1(A).
We also have the quasi-arc-symmetric analog of proposition 9.6.63:
115


## Page 116


Proposition 9.6.73. Let N be a compact Nash submanifold of M and S be a subset of N. The
set S is quasi-arc-symmetric in the Nash manifold N if and only if S is quasi-arc-symmetric
in M.
Proof. Suppose that S is quasi-arc-symmetric in N and let γ :] −1; 1[→M be an analytic
arc such that γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂S ⊂N: since N is AS-closed in M, we have γ(] −1; 1[) ⊂N
and we can restrict γ into an arc eγ :] −1; 1[→N, which is analytic with respect to the real
analytic manifold N since N is an analytic submanifold of M. As a consequence, because S
is quasi-arc-symmetric in N and eγ(] −1; 0[) = γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂S, there exists ϵ ∈]0; 1[ such that
eγ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂S i.e. γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂S.
The converse implication is given by previous lemma 9.6.72 applied to the inclusion map
N ,→M.

Definition 9.6.75. Let S be a subset of M. We say that S is an AS-set of M if S is a finite
Boolean combination of semialgebraic arc-symmetric subsets of M.
Remark that the class of AS-sets of M is stable under finite Boolean combination by
definition and that, by proposition 9.6.69, an AS-set of M is quasi-arc-symmetric in M. We
are going to show that the converse is true when M is a real affine space. Thereafter, we will
show the equivalence of the two notions when M is supposed compact and affine, using the
following analogs of lemma 9.6.72 and proposition 9.6.73:
Lemma 9.6.76.
1. If f is a semialgebraic arc-analytic map from an AS-closed set S of M to a semialgebraic
set T of a Nash manifold N and A is an AS-set of N included in T, then the set f−1(A)
is an AS-set of M.
2. If N is a compact Nash submanifold of M and S is a subset of N, then S is an AS-set
of N if and only if S is an AS-set of M.

Corollary 9.6.78. Suppose that M is a compact affine Nash manifold. Then a subset of M
is quasi-arc-symmetric in M if and only if it is an AS-set of M.
Proof. Let Φ be an affine Nash embedding of M onto a Nash submanifold N of a real affine
space Rs (notice that N is compact as well) and let S be a subset of M. Then S is quasi-arc-
symmetric in M if and only if Φ(S) is quasi-arc-symmetric in N (lemma 9.6.72) if and only
if Φ(S) is quasi-arc-symmetric in Rs (proposition 9.6.73) if and only if Φ(S) is an AS-set of Rs
(proposition 9.6.77) if and only if Φ(S) is an AS-set of N (lemma 9.6.76 2) if and only if S is
an AS-set M (lemma 9.6.76 1).

Example 9.6.80. The real projective space Pn(R) is a compact affine Nash manifold (any real
projective space, with its usual atlas, is actually a compact affine nonsingular real algebraic
variety: see section 3.4 of [3]). As a consequence, the quasi-arc-symmetric subsets of Pn(R)
are AS-sets and we recover the characterization of Proposition 3.2 of [16], as well as Proposi-
tion 3.3 (by lemma 9.6.74, remark 9.6.79 2 above and lemma 9.6.53).
Let us also show the following characterization of AS-sets of real affine spaces or compact
affine Nash manifolds that we will use in next paragraph:
Lemma 9.6.81. Suppose that M is Rn or a compact affine Nash manifold and let S be a
semialgebraic subset of M. Then S is an AS-set of M if and only if for any injective Nash
arc γ :] −1; 1[→M, if γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂S, then there exists ϵ ∈]0; 1[ such that γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂S.
Proof. Remark that, if we replace analytic arcs with injective Nash arcs in the definition of
quasi-arc-symmetric sets, proposition 9.6.69 remains true as well as lemma 9.6.72 for injective
semialgebraic arc-analytic maps, and then proposition 9.6.73 is also true.
Now, let B be a closed semialgebraic subset of M satisfying the condition of the lemma. If
M = Rn, let Ψ denote the identity map on Rn, and if M is a compact affine Nash manifold,
let Ψ be an affine Nash embedding of M onto a compact Nash submanifold of an affine space Rs.
In both cases, the closed semialgebraic subset Ψ(B) of Rn, resp. Rs, satisfies, by the above
considerations, the same condition as a subset of Rn, resp. Rs, and is then, according to the
proof of lemma 9.6.68 and the characterization of AR-closed sets of Rs of lemma 9.6.9, an
AR-closed set of Rn, resp. Rs. By propositions 9.6.63 and 9.6.64, B is therefore an AS-closed
set of M. This proves the “injective Nash arcs” version of lemma 9.6.68 which implies the
corresponding analog of lemma 9.6.74.
As a result, we can use the same arguments as in the proof of proposition 9.6.77 to assert
the equivalence of the lemma: see remark 9.6.79 2.
In the last subsection, we will focus on AS-sets of real projective spaces, as in section 3
of [16]. Before, let us consider maps between AS-sets of compact affine Nash manifolds which
have AS-graph.
9.6.9
AS-maps
In the following, we will consider only compact affine Nash manifolds, on which we proved that
quasi-arc-symmetric sets and AS-sets form one single notion: in accordance with [16], we will
just speak of AS-sets in this context.
So suppose that M is a compact affine Nash manifold and let N be a compact affine
Nash manifold. We are going to study maps between AS-sets of M and N which generalize
semialgebraic arc-analytic maps between AS-closed sets:
118


## Page 119


Definition 9.6.82. Let S be an AS-set of M, T be an AS-set of N and f : S →T. We
say that f is an AS-map if its graph Γf is an AS-set of M × N (notice that the Cartesian
product M × N is a compact affine Nash manifold as well).
Examples 9.6.83.
1. By proposition 9.6.64 1, a semialgebraic arc-analytic map from an AS-closed set of M to
an AS-closed set of N is an AS-map.
2. If f : S →T is an AS-map between respective AS-sets S and T of M and N, if B
is an AS-set of M included in S and A is an AS-set of N included in T such that
f(B) ⊂A, then the map ef : B →A ; x 7→f(x) induced by f is an AS-map as well,
since Γ ef = Γf ∩(B × A) (see remark 9.6.71).
Remarks 9.6.84. Keep the notations of definition 9.6.82.
1. If f is a bijective AS-map, then the inverse f−1 : T →S of f is an AS-map as well,
since the graph Γf−1 of f−1 is the image of Γf by the Nash diffeomorphism M × N →
N × M ; (x, y) 7→(y, x) (see lemma 9.6.72 or lemma 9.6.76 1).
2. If Φ is a Nash embedding of M into a real affine space Rn and Ψ is a Nash embedding
of N into an affine space Rm, then the map f is an AS-map if and only if the map
ef := Ψ◦f ◦Φ−1 : Φ(S) →Ψ(T) is an AS-map if and only if the graph Γ ef = (Φ×Ψ) (Γf)
of ef is an AS-set of Rn+m (see lemma 9.6.72 or lemma 9.6.76 1).
We established in previous paragraph that the class of AS-sets of compact affine Nash
manifolds is stable under inverse image by semialgebraic arc-analytic maps from an AS-closed
set to a semialgebraic set. In fact, it is more generally stable under inverse image by AS-maps:
we will show this result after having proved that the image of an AS-set by an injective AS-map
is an AS-set as well.

Corollary 9.6.86. Let S be an AS-set of M, T be an AS-set of N and f : S →T be
an AS-map. If A is an AS-set of N included in T, then f−1(A) is an AS-set of M.
Proof. We adapt the proof of Proposition 2.2.7 of [3]: the set f−1(A) is the image of (S×A)∩Γf
by the map π : (S × A) ∩Γf →T ; (x, y) 7→x. On the one hand, the former set is an AS-set
of M × N by definition and by remark 9.6.71. On the other hand, the map π is injective and is
moreover an AS-map, as the restriction of the Nash projection map M × N →N ; (x, y) 7→x
(see examples 9.6.83). Therefore, f−1(A) is an AS-set of M by theorem 9.6.85.

(induced by the Nash projection map M × N × L →M × L ; (x, y, z) 7→(x, z)), so that we

9.6.10
AS-sets and AS-maps of real projective spaces
In this final part, we will consider the compact affine Nash manifold Pn(R) (see example 9.6.80):
this is the framework of section 3 of [16].
First, notice the following examples of AS-sets of Pn(R): recall that the Zariski-closed
sets of the real algebraic variety Pn(R) are the projective algebraic sets of Pn(R) (see for
instance section 3.4.1 of [3]) and denote by Φ : Pn(R) →P the biregular isomorphism given
in section 3.4.2 of [3] sending Pn(R) onto a (connected irreducible) compact nonsingular real
algebraic set (and then compact Nash submanifold) of R(n+1)2.
Lemma 9.6.89. Any projective algebraic subset of Pn(R) is AS-closed in Pn(R). Any real
algebraic set of Rn is an AS-set of Pn(R).
Proof. Let Z be a projective algebraic subset of Pn(R), then Φ(Z) is a real algebraic set
of R(n+1)2 contained in the compact Nash submanifold P: by example 9.6.2 and proposi-
tions 9.6.63 and 9.6.64, Z is therefore a semialgebraic arc-symmetric set of Pn(R).
Now, let X be a real algebraic subset of Rn: if (x1, . . . , xn) denotes the coordinates of Rn
and [x0 : x1 : . . . : xn] denotes the homogeneous coordinates of Pn(R), we identify Rn with
the Zariski-open subset {x0 ̸= 0} of Pn(R). The set X is then a Zariski-open set of Pn(R) as
the set-theoretic difference of the projective algebraic set obtained by homogenizing generators
of the ideal I(X) and the hyperplane at infinity of equation x0 = 0. Therefore, by the above
showed statement, X is an AS-set of Pn(R) as the set-theoretic difference of two AS-closed
sets of Pn(R).
Remark 9.6.90. More generally, any connected component of a projective algebraic subset
of Pn(R) is a semialgebraic arc-symmetric set of Pn(R), using the same argument as above
together with example 9.6.4 2 (recall that the connected components of a semialgebraic subset
of a real affine space are semialgebraic: cf. Theorem 2.4.5 of [3]).
Even if all real algebraic sets, as well as all Zariski-open sets, of Rn are AS-sets of Pn(R),
this is not the case for any AS-set of Rn:
Example 9.6.91. Recall the example of remark 9.6.41: the positive half-branch C of the hy-

On the other hand, a compact subset of Rn is AS-closed in Rn if and only if it is AS-closed
in Pn(R) (see also Remark 3.5 of [16]):
Proposition 9.6.92. Let S be compact subset of Rn. Then S is semialgebraic arc-symmetric
in Pn(R) if and only if S is semialgebraic arc-symmetric in Rn.
Proof. Notice first that S is semialgebraic in Pn(R) if and only if S is semialgebraic in Rn:
consider the Nash inclusion map i : Rn ,→Pn(R) (Rn is a Nash submanifold of the Nash
manifold Pn(R)) and apply for instance lemma 9.6.45 3 (this does not use the compactness
hypothesis on S).
Now, suppose that S is arc-symmetric in Pn(R), then S = i−1(S) is arc-symmetric in Rn
by proposition 9.6.39 2 (this is also independent of the compactness hypothesis on S).
Conversely, suppose that S is arc-symmetric in Rn and let γ :]−1; 1[→Pn(R) be an analytic
arc such that γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂S ⊂Rn. On the one hand, since Rn is an AS-set of Pn(R) (lemma
9.6.89), there exists ϵ ∈]0; 1[ such that γ(]0; ϵ[) ⊂Rn. On the other hand, since S is compact, S
is closed in Pn(R) and then γ(0) ∈S ⊂Rn. As a consequence, γ induces the analytic arc
eγ : t ∈] −1; ϵ[7→γ(t) ∈Rn and, since eγ(] −1; 0[) = γ(] −1; 0[) ⊂S and S is arc-symmetric in
Rn, we have γ(] −1; ϵ[) = eγ(] −1; ϵ[) ⊂S, so that S is arc-symmetric in Pn(R).
Example 9.6.93. Any compact connected component of an AR-closed set of Rn (e.g. of a real
algebraic set of Rn) is AS-closed in Pn(R): cf. remark 9.6.7 (see also example 9.6.4 2) as well
as Theorem 2.4.5 of [3].

Remark 9.6.95. Up to Nash diffeomorphism, any AS-set of a compact affine Nash manifold
can be considered as an AS-set of a real projective space: if S is a subset of a compact Nash
manifold M with affine Nash embedding Ψ onto a Nash submanifold L of Rn, then S is an
AS-set of M if and only if Ψ(S) is an AS-set of Rn (cf. for instance lemma 9.6.76) if and only
if Ψ(S) is an AS-set of Pn(R) (the Euclidean-closed AR-closure of Ψ(S) in Rn is contained in
the compact AR-closed set L of Rn, so that Ψ(S)
AR is compact in Rn as well).
If m ∈N\{0}, the real affine space Rn+m = Rn ×Rm can also be considered as a Nash sub-
manifold of the product Nash manifold Pn(R) × Pm(R). We then prove the following analog of
previous corollary 9.6.94 that gives, in particular, a sufficient condition for a map between AS-
sets of real projectives spaces included in affine spaces to be an AS-map.
Proposition 9.6.96. Let S be a subset of Rn+m such that the AR-closure S
AR of S in Rn+m
is compact. Then S is an AS-set of Pn(R) × Pm(R) if and only if S is an AS-set of Rn+m.
Proof. Considering the Nash inclusion map Rn+m ,→Pn(R)×Pm(R), the set S is semialgebraic
in Pn(R)×Pm(R) if and only if S is semialgebraic in Rn+m (cf. proposition 9.6.39 2). Moreover,
if (y1, . . . , ym) denotes the coordinates of Rm and [y0 : y1 : . . . : ym] denotes the homogeneous
coordinates of Pm(R), we have
Rn+m =

and Rn+m is therefore an AS-set of Pn(R) × Pm(R) (use lemma 9.6.89 and, for instance,
remark 9.6.71). As a consequence, we can use the same arguments as in the proofs of proposi-
tion 9.6.92 and corollary 9.6.94 to assert the desired equivalence.
Corollary 9.6.97. Let S be an AS-set of Pn(R) included in Rn, T be an AS-set of Pm(R)
included in Rm and f : S →T be a map. If the AR-closure of Γf in Rn+m is compact, then f
is an AS-map if and only if Γf is an AS-set of Rn+m.
Proof. It is a direct application of proposition 9.6.96.
Example 9.6.98. Keep the notations of previous corollary 9.6.97. If the respective AR-closures
of S and T in Rn and Rm are compact, the graph Γf of f satisfies the condition of the statement
since Γf

in S

In particular, if B and A are respective real algebraic subsets of Rn and Rm and f : B →A
is a map with algebraic graph (i.e. the graph Gf of f is a real algebraic set of Rn+m), then f
is an AS-map between the AS-sets B ⊂Pn(R) and A ⊂Pm(R).
